. For the variance, we have that

\mathrm{Var}(h(\widehat{q}_{m}))=\alpha^{2}\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{q}_{m})=\alpha^{2}\sigma_{X}^{2}/m

exactly.

(i) Bias.
Apply Taylor’s theorem with Lagrange remainder to
h
at
q
to get that

h(\widehat{q}_{m})=h(q)+h^{\prime}(q)(\widehat{q}_{m}-q)+\tfrac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}(q)(\widehat{q}_{m}-q)^{2}+R_{m},

(84)

where
|R_{m}|\leq\tfrac{1}{6}\|h^{\prime\prime\prime}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{N}}|\widehat{q}_{m}-q|^{3}

provided
\widehat{q}_{m}\in\mathcal{N}
.

By Höeffding’s inequality (since
X_{i}\in[a,b]
):

\Pr(|\widehat{q}_{m}-q|>t)\leq 2\exp(-2mt^{2}/(b-a)^{2})
,
so
\widehat{q}_{m}\in\mathcal{N}
holds with probability
\geq 1-2e^{-cm}
for
some
c>0
depending only on the radius of
\mathcal{N}
and
(b-a)
.

We now take the expectation of (
84
). The first-order term is

\E[h^{\prime}(q)(\widehat{q}_{m}-q)]=h^{\prime}(q)\E[\widehat{q}_{m}-q]=0
.
The second-order term is

\E[\tfrac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}(q)(\widehat{q}_{m}-q)^{2}]=\tfrac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}(q)\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{q}_{m})=h^{\prime\prime}(q)\sigma_{X}^{2}/(2m)
.
The remainder, restricted to
\{\widehat{q}_{m}\in\mathcal{N}\}
is given by

\E|R_{m}\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{q}_{m}\in\mathcal{N}}|\leq\tfrac{1}{6}\|h^{\prime\prime\prime}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{N}}\E|\widehat{q}_{m}-q|^{3}=O(m^{-3/2})

by direct calculation of the third absolute moment of a sample mean of bounded
random variables (Marcinkiewicz-Zygmund or moment bounds for
[a,b]
-valued sums).
The contribution from
\{\widehat{q}_{m}\notin\mathcal{N}\}
is bounded by

\sup_{\mathbb{R}}|h|\cdot 2e^{-cm}=o(m^{-3/2})
assuming
h
is bounded; if
h

is only locally bounded (as for
\log q
), use truncation arguments which
also give
o(m^{-3/2})
.

Combining these bounds, we have that
\E[h(\widehat{q}_{m})]-h(q)=h^{\prime\prime}(q)\sigma_{X}^{2}/(2m)+O(m^{-3/2})
,
which is (
81
). The bound (
82
)
follows from Popoviciu’s inequality
\sigma_{X}^{2}\leq(b-a)^{2}/4
.

(ii) Variance.
By the same Taylor expansion applied to

h(\widehat{q}_{m})-\E[h(\widehat{q}_{m})]
, we have that

h(\widehat{q}_{m})-\E[h(\widehat{q}_{m})]=h^{\prime}(q)(\widehat{q}_{m}-q)+\widetilde{R}_{m},

where
\widetilde{R}_{m}
is centered and satisfies
\E|\widetilde{R}_{m}|^{2}=O(m^{-2})

by similar moment bounds. Taking the variance gives

\mathrm{Var}(h(\widehat{q}_{m}))=h^{\prime}(q)^{2}\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{q}_{m})+\E[\widetilde{R}_{m}^{2}]+2\E[h^{\prime}(q)(\widehat{q}_{m}-q)\widetilde{R}_{m}].

The first term is
h^{\prime}(q)^{2}\sigma_{X}^{2}/m
. The second is
O(m^{-2})
. The
third is bounded by Cauchy-Schwarz:

|2\E[h^{\prime}(q)(\widehat{q}_{m}-q)\widetilde{R}_{m}]|\leq 2|h^{\prime}(q)|\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{q}_{m})\E\widetilde{R}_{m}^{2}}=O(m^{-3/2}).

Combining these bounds gives us
\mathrm{Var}(h(\widehat{q}_{m}))=h^{\prime}(q)^{2}\sigma_{X}^{2}/m+O(m^{-2})
,
which is (
83
).
∎

G.4
Examples

Let us now present examples to help concretize ideas.

Entropic risk.

The per-component bias and variance of the entropic risk are easily characterized via the following proposition.

Proposition 7

(Per-component bias and variance: entropic risk)
.

Let
\rho_{\lambda}(X)=-\lambda^{-1}\log\E[e^{-\lambda X}]
be the entropic
risk at parameter
\lambda>0
for
X\in[0,1]
, with plug-in estimator

\widehat{\rho}_{\lambda}^{m}:=-\lambda^{-1}\log(m^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}e^{-\lambda X_{i}})
.
Then

\displaystyle\bigl|\E[\widehat{\rho}_{\lambda}^{m}]-\rho_{\lambda}(X)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda})^{2}}{8\lambda\,e^{-2\lambda}\,m}+O(m^{-3/2}),

(85)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{\rho}_{\lambda}^{m})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda})^{2}}{4\lambda^{2}\,e^{-2\lambda}\,m}+O(m^{-2}).

(86)

Proof.

Set
Y:=e^{-\lambda X}
. Since
X\in[0,1]
, we have that
Y\in[e^{-\lambda},1]
,
so
(b-a)
for
Y
is
(1-e^{-\lambda})
. Set
h(q):=-\lambda^{-1}\log q
,
so
\widehat{\rho}_{\lambda}^{m}=h(\widehat{q}_{m})
where
\widehat{q}_{m}=m^{-1}\sum_{i}Y_{i}

and
\E\widehat{q}_{m}=q:=\E Y\in[e^{-\lambda},1]
.

Computing derivatives, we have that
h^{\prime}(q)=-(\lambda q)^{-1}
,
h^{\prime\prime}(q)=(\lambda q^{2})^{-1}
.
On the range
q\in[e^{-\lambda},1]
, we have that

|h^{\prime}(q)|\leq(\lambda e^{-\lambda})^{-1}=e^{\lambda}/\lambda\quad\text{and}\quad|h^{\prime\prime}(q)|\leq(\lambda e^{-2\lambda})^{-1}=e^{2\lambda}/\lambda.

Apply Lemma
6
(i) with
C_{h}^{\prime\prime}=e^{2\lambda}/\lambda

and
(b-a)=1-e^{-\lambda}
to get that

\bigl|\E[\widehat{\rho}_{\lambda}^{m}]-\rho_{\lambda}\bigr|\leq\frac{e^{2\lambda}}{\lambda}\cdot\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda})^{2}}{8m}+O(m^{-3/2})=\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda})^{2}e^{2\lambda}}{8\lambda m}+O(m^{-3/2}),

which equals the right-hand side of (
85
).

Apply Lemma
6
(ii) to get that

\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{\rho}_{\lambda}^{m})\leq|h^{\prime}(q)|^{2}\frac{\sigma_{Y}^{2}}{m}+O(m^{-2})\leq\frac{e^{2\lambda}}{\lambda^{2}}\cdot\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda})^{2}}{4m}+O(m^{-2}),

which equals (
86
).
∎

Remark 1

(Constants for entropic)
.

For convenience, define

C_{b}^{\rm ent}(\lambda):=\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda})^{2}e^{2\lambda}}{8\lambda},\qquad C_{\zeta}^{\rm ent}(\lambda):=\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda})^{2}e^{2\lambda}}{4\lambda^{2}},

(87)

so per-component bias is
\leq C_{b}^{\rm ent}/m+\mathcal{O}(m^{-3/2})
and per-component
variance is
\leq C_{\zeta}^{\rm ent}/m+\mathcal{O}(m^{-2})
. Both blow up as
\lambda

grows; this reflects the increasing ”concentration on the worst case” of
the entropic functional, which is harder to estimate.

CVaR via Rockafellar-Uryasev.

The per-component bias and variance of CVaR are easily characterized via the following proposition which utilizes the well-known Rockafellar-Uryasev transformation
49
.

Proposition 8

(Per-component bias of CVaR-RU)
.

The CVaR at level
\alpha\in(0,1)
admits the variational representation

\mathrm{CVaR}_{\alpha}(X)=\inf_{\nu\in\mathbb{R}}\Bigl\{\nu+(1-\alpha)^{-1}\E[(X-\nu)_{+}]\Bigr\},

(88)

attained at
\nu^{\star}=\mathrm{VaR}_{\alpha}(X)
. Define the empirical
estimator at fixed
\nu
as

\widehat{C}_{m}(\nu):=\nu+(1-\alpha)^{-1}m^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(X_{i}-\nu)_{+}.

i.

For each fixed
\nu
,
\widehat{C}_{m}(\nu)
is unbiased for its
population value
\nu+(1-\alpha)^{-1}\E[(X-\nu)_{+}]
. At

\nu=\nu^{\star}
,
\E[\widehat{C}_{m}(\nu^{\star})]=\mathrm{CVaR}_{\alpha}(X)
.

ii.

When
\nu
is treated as an additional decision variable optimized
jointly with the policy parameters via the variational form
(
88
), the value
\widehat{C}_{m}
at the joint optimum is
unbiased for
\mathrm{CVaR}_{\alpha}
.

iii.

The per-component variance is

\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{C}_{m}(\nu^{\star}))=(1-\alpha)^{-2}\mathrm{Var}((X-\nu^{\star})_{+})/m\leq(1-\alpha)^{-2}/(4m)
,
since
X\in[0,1]
implies
(X-\nu^{\star})_{+}\in[0,1]
.

Proof.

We prove each of the claims in order.

(i)
For fixed
\nu
,
\widehat{C}_{m}(\nu)
is the sample mean of the linear
functional
X\mapsto\nu+(1-\alpha)^{-1}(X-\nu)_{+}
. By
Lemma
6
(iii), it is unbiased for the population value.
At
\nu=\nu^{\star}
, the population value equals
\mathrm{CVaR}_{\alpha}(X)
by
(
88
).

(ii)
When
\nu
is jointly optimized, denote the joint optimum by

(\theta^{\star},\nu^{\star}_{m})
. At the joint optimum,
\nu^{\star}_{m}
is
chosen to satisfy the optimality condition for the inner
\inf
in
(
88
), evaluated with the empirical objective. Since the
empirical objective is unbiased per (i), the resulting
\widehat{C}_{m}
at the
joint optimum is unbiased.
4
4

4

A subtle point: the joint optimization
introduces correlation between
\nu^{\star}_{m}
(random, depends on samples)
and the empirical sum. At the population optimum
\nu^{\star}
, both

\widehat{C}_{m}(\nu^{\star})
and
\E[\widehat{C}_{m}(\nu^{\star})]
equal

\mathrm{CVaR}_{\alpha}(X)
in expectation, so the bias is zero in the limit. In
finite samples,
\nu^{\star}_{m}
has finite-sample bias from the empirical
quantile estimation; this is
O(1/m)
for smooth distributions, but vanishes
at the population optimum and does not enter the convergence rate at first
order. See Rockafellar-Uryasev (2000) for the optimization-equivalence
argument.

(iii)
Linear functional with
X\in[0,1]
, so
(X-\nu^{\star})_{+}\in[0,1]

with variance
\leq 1/4
by Popoviciu. Sample-mean variance is

(1-\alpha)^{-2}\mathrm{Var}((X-\nu^{\star})_{+})/m\leq(1-\alpha)^{-2}/(4m)
.
∎

Remark 2

(Constants for CVaR-RU)
.

The constants for CVaR-RU are given by

C_{b}^{\rm CVaR-RU}=0,\qquad C_{\zeta}^{\rm CVaR-RU}(\alpha)=\frac{1}{4(1-\alpha)^{2}}.

(89)

The bias is exactly zero at the population optimum, which is the cleanest
case. Variance grows as
\alpha\to 1
(estimating the extreme tail
requires more samples).

G.5
Aggregate bounds: (O1)-(O2) for the operator

Proposition 9

(Operator-level bias and variance)
.

Let
\widehat{F}_{\mathcal{R},m}
be the empirical operator (
76
) for
the risk-adjusted preference operator with samples
Y^{\prime\prime}_{1},\ldots,Y^{\prime\prime}_{m}\sim\pi_{\theta}
.

(i)

Entropic risk at parameter
\lambda
.
Assumption
(O1)

holds with

B_{m}^{\rm ent}:=\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{Y}|}\,C_{b}^{\rm ent}(\lambda)}{\beta\,m}+O(m^{-3/2}).

(90)

Assumption
(O2)
holds with

V_{m}^{\rm ent}:=\frac{|\mathcal{Y}|\,C_{\zeta}^{\rm ent}(\lambda)}{\beta^{2}\,m}+O(m^{-2}).

(91)

(ii)

CVaR at level
\alpha
via Rockafellar-Uryasev.
Assumption

(O1)
holds with
B_{m}^{\rm CVaR-RU}=0
. Assumption
(O2)

holds with

V_{m}^{\rm CVaR-RU}:=\frac{|\mathcal{Y}|}{4\beta^{2}(1-\alpha)^{2}\,m}.

(92)

Both sets of bounds are uniform in
\theta
(and hence in
\pi_{\theta}
):
the constants depend only on the risk parameter and the boundedness of

P(y\succ Y^{\prime\prime})\in[0,1]
, not on
\pi_{\theta}
.

Proof.

By the decomposition (
76
)-(
77
), we have that

\|b_{m}(\theta)\|_{2}^{2}=\beta^{-2}\sum_{y}\bigl|\E[\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathcal{R},m}(\pi_{\theta})_{y}]-\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{R}}(\pi_{\theta})_{y}\bigr|^{2}

and

\E\|\zeta_{m}(\theta)\|_{2}^{2}=\beta^{-2}\sum_{y}\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathcal{R},m}(\pi_{\theta})_{y})
.
For entropic, apply Proposition
7
per-component to conclude that
each per-component bias is
\leq C_{b}^{\rm ent}/m+\mathcal{O}(m^{-3/2})
and each
per-component variance is
\leq C_{\zeta}^{\rm ent}/m+\mathcal{O}(m^{-2})
. Summing
over
|\mathcal{Y}|
components (worst case) and taking square root for the bias
gives (
90
). Summing variances gives (
91
).

For CVaR-RU, apply Proposition
8
: per-component bias is 0
(at the joint optimum), so
\|b_{m}\|_{2}=0
giving
B_{m}^{\rm CVaR-RU}=0
.
Per-component variance is less than or equal to
1/(4(1-\alpha)^{2}m)
, and summing gives
(
92
).

Uniformity in
\pi_{\theta}
yields the fact that constants
C_{b}^{\rm ent},C_{\zeta}^{\rm ent}

depend only on
\lambda
and on the range
X\in[0,1]
(which is independent
of
\pi_{\theta}
), not on
\pi_{\theta}
itself. Same for CVaR.
∎

Looseness of the bounds.

The bounds in Proposition
9
are loose in two ways:

1.

Worst-case per-component aggregation.
We took the per-component
bound and multiplied by
|\mathcal{Y}|
, ignoring the possibility that not every
component achieves the maximum. In practice, for many distributions

\pi_{\theta}
, the per-component variances are much smaller than the worst case.

2.

Off-diagonal covariance ignored.
The bound on
\E\|\zeta_{m}\|_{2}^{2}

uses only diagonal variances, which is exact for the
\ell_{2}
norm. However,
for the
\Sigma^{+}
norm used in the convergence proofs, we apply the loose
bound
\E\|\zeta_{m}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}\E\|\zeta_{m}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}V_{m}

(see Section
H.1
), which incurs an additional factor of

\sigma_{\max}
. A tighter bound via direct computation of

\E\|\zeta_{m}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}=\mathrm{tr}(\Sigma^{+}\mathrm{Cov}(\zeta_{m}))
would exploit the
full covariance structure of the empirical risk estimator and the spectrum
of
\Sigma^{+}
; this could improve the variance constant by a factor up to

\kappa_{\Sigma}=\sigma_{\max}/\sigma_{\min}
. We do not pursue this
refinement here.

The constants given suffice for establishing the convergence rates with
explicit
1/m
scaling on the bias and variance floors.

Appendix H
Stochastic Convergence Analysis with Bias

We prove convergence of stochastic projected Mirror-Prox (extragradient) on
the IPO gradient flow with a biased stochastic oracle, in the

\Sigma^{+}
-weighted Euclidean geometry on
\mathcal{W}
. The proof structure
follows
34
with several core modifications that arise from handling the risk case. We also write the theorems and lemmas in our Euclidean setting; all proofs are self-contained.
We choose to provide proofs for extra-gradient as opposed to mirror descent even in the monotone case because there are benefits to running extragradient: though it requires an extra step, it will handle corrections for cycling, and potentially cover the case in practice where the estimated game operator fails to be strongly monotone.

H.1
Preliminaries

Let
F_{\mathcal{R}}:\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the risk-adjusted game
operator on the affine slice
\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
. As is typical, we require some regularity assumptions. Not all are assumed in all results or throughout; instead we call on different elements of the list where needed.

Assumption 2
.

The following regularity properties hold:

a.

(P-mono)

Monotonicity.
For all
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
, the lower bound holds:

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2}),\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\rangle\geq 0.

b.

(P-strong)

Strong monotonicity (when assumed).
The map
F_{\mathcal{R}}
is
\mu_{\mathcal{R}}
-strongly
monotone on
\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
:

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2}),\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\rangle\geq\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2}^{2},

with
\mu_{\mathcal{R}}>0
. When this holds,
(P-mono)
is implied.

c.

(P-lip)

Lipschitz Continuity.
The map
F_{\mathcal{R}}
is
\ell_{\mathcal{R}}
-Lipschitz continuous on
\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
:

\|F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2})\|_{2}\leq\ell_{\mathcal{R}}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2}.

The IPO gradient is
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\rm IPO}(\theta)=2\Sigma F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta)
. Let

G(\theta):=\Sigma F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta)

be the operator we analyze where the factor of two is absorbed into the step size.

On
\mathcal{W}
, define
\langle u,v\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}:=u^{\top}\Sigma^{+}v
and

\|u\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}:=u^{\top}\Sigma^{+}u
, where
\Sigma^{+}
is the Moore-Penrose
pseudoinverse, positive definite on
\mathcal{W}
with eigenvalues

1/\sigma_{\max},\ldots,1/\sigma_{\min}
. Norm equivalence on
\mathcal{W}
implies

\sigma_{\min}\|v\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\|v\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}\|v\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(93)

Risk-adjusted Psuedo-gradient Operator.

Let
F_{\mathcal{R}}:\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the risk-adjusted game
operator, assumed monotone and
\ell_{\mathcal{R}}
-Lipschitz on
\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
:
for all
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
,

\displaystyle\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2}),\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq 0,\quad\text{and}\quad\|F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2})\|_{2}\leq\ell_{\mathcal{R}}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2}.

(94)

Lemma 7

(Lipschitz continuity of
F_{\mathcal{R}}
)
.

The map

F_{\mathcal{R}}
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{Y}|}
with
constant
\ell_{\mathcal{R}}\leq\beta+L_{\mathcal{R}}
, where
L_{\mathcal{R}}
is the Lipschitz
constant of
\mu\mapsto P_{\mathcal{R}}(\mu)
as a map between
\Delta(\mathcal{Y})

and
\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{Y}|}
.

Proof.

We derive directly from the definition of
F_{\mathcal{R}}
, the sequence of bounds

\displaystyle\|F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2}+\|P_{\mathcal{R}}(\pi_{1})-P_{\mathcal{R}}(\pi_{2})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2}+L_{\mathcal{R}}\|\pi_{1}-\pi_{2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\beta+L_{\mathcal{R}})\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2},

using that
\theta\mapsto\pi_{\theta}
(softmax) is 1-Lipschitz from
\ell_{2}

to
\ell_{2}
.
∎

H.2
Properties of
G=\Sigma F_{\mathcal{R}}
in the
\Sigma^{+}
metric

The next two lemmas establish that
G
inherits monotonicity and Lipschitz
properties from
F_{\mathcal{R}}
when measured in the
\Sigma^{+}
inner product.

Lemma 8

(Preconditioning preserves monotonicity in
\Sigma^{+}
metric)
.

For any
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
, the following two relationships hold:

(i)

\langle G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2}),\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2}),\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\rangle_{2}
, and
(ii)

\|G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}\|F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2})\|_{2}^{2}
.
Consequently, we have that

a.

Under
(P-mono)
, the map
G
is monotone in the
\Sigma^{+}
inner product.

b.

Under
(P-strong)
, the map
G
is
\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\sigma_{\min}
-strongly monotone in the
\Sigma^{+}
inner product:

\langle G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2}),\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\geq\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\sigma_{\min}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
.

Proof.

Let
\Delta:=\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\in\mathcal{W}
and
\xi:=F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2})
.
Note
G(\theta_{i})=\Sigma F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{i})
, so
G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2})=\Sigma\xi
.

Proof for claim (i).
We directly compute

\langle G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2}),\Delta\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle\Sigma\xi,\Delta\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\Delta^{\top}\Sigma^{+}\Sigma\xi=\Delta^{\top}\Pi_{\mathcal{W}}\xi,

where
\Pi_{\mathcal{W}}=\Sigma^{+}\Sigma
is the orthogonal projector onto
\mathcal{W}

(in the standard inner product), using the Moore-Penrose property

\Sigma^{+}\Sigma=\Pi_{\mathrm{range}(\Sigma)}=\Pi_{\mathcal{W}}
. Since
\Delta\in\mathcal{W}
,

\Delta^{\top}\Pi_{\mathcal{W}}\xi=\Delta^{\top}\xi=\langle\xi,\Delta\rangle_{2}
.

Proof for claim (ii).
Analogously, we compute

\|G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}=(\Sigma\xi)^{\top}\Sigma^{+}(\Sigma\xi)=\xi^{\top}\Sigma\Sigma^{+}\Sigma\xi=\xi^{\top}\Sigma\xi,

using
\Sigma\Sigma^{+}\Sigma=\Sigma
(Moore-Penrose). Then

\xi^{\top}\Sigma\xi\leq\sigma_{\max}\|\xi\|_{2}^{2}
by the eigenvalue bound on
\Sigma
.

Proof for claim a.
By
(i)
, under
(P-mono)
, we deduce that

\langle G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2}),\Delta\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle\xi,\Delta\rangle_{2}\geq 0
.

Proof for claim b.
By
(ii)
, under
(P-strong)
, we further deduce that

\langle G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2}),\Delta\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle\xi,\Delta\rangle_{2}\geq\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\sigma_{\min}\|\Delta\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
,
the last inequality from (
93
).
∎

Lemma 9

(Lipschitz constant of
G
in
\Sigma^{+}
metric)
.

Under (P-lip),
G
is
L_{G}
-Lipschitz in the
\Sigma^{+}
inner product on
\mathcal{W}

with

L_{G}:=\sigma_{\max}\ell_{\mathcal{R}}
.
That is, for all
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
, the upper bound holds:

\|G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq L_{G}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8
part
(ii)
, we have that

\|G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}\|F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2})\|_{2}^{2}.

By
(P-lip)
, we have that

\|F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\ell_{\mathcal{R}}^{2}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2}^{2}.

By (
93
), we also have that

\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

Combining these three bounds, we deduce that

\|G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\ell_{\mathcal{R}}^{2}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

Taking the square root gives us the conclusion—indeed, we see that
\|G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\sigma_{\max}\ell_{\mathcal{R}}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}=L_{G}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}
.
∎

H.3
Distance-generating function and projection

Choose

D:=\{\theta\in\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}:\|\theta-\theta_{\rm ref}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq R\}

(95)

for
R>0
to be determined by the boundedness lemma.
Following
34
, we use the distance generating function
\omega(\theta)=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\theta-\theta_{\rm ref}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}

on the slice
\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
. This is strongly convex with modulus
one with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{\Sigma^{+}}
. The associated prox-function (Bregman
divergence) is

V(z,u):=\omega(u)-\omega(z)-\langle\nabla\omega(z),u-z\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\|z-u\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

The prox-mapping is

P_{z}(\xi):=\arg\min_{u\in D}\{V(z,u)+\langle\xi,u\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\}=\Pi_{D}(z-\xi),

where
\Pi_{D}
is the Euclidean projection onto
D
in the
\Sigma^{+}
metric.

The prox-center of the projection set
D
is

z_{c}=\theta_{\rm ref}
. Again, following
34
, we have that

\displaystyle\Theta(z_{c})

\displaystyle:=\max_{u\in D}V(z_{c},u)=\tfrac{1}{2}R^{2},\quad\text{and}\quad\Omega:=\sqrt{2\Theta(z_{c})}=R.

(96)

Thus
D\subseteq\{\theta:\|\theta-z_{c}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\Omega\}
,
i.e., the
\Sigma^{+}
-radius of
D
from
z_{c}
is exactly
\Omega=R
. The

\Sigma^{+}
-diameter of
D
is at most
2\Omega=2R
.

Lemma 10

(Boundedness of equilibrium in
\Sigma^{+}
)
.

The risk-adjusted equilibrium
\theta^{\star}\in\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
satisfies

\|\theta^{\star}-\theta_{\rm ref}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{Y}|}}{\beta\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}.

Proof.

The equilibrium condition
F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta^{\star})\in\ker\Sigma
on the slice

\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
means

\beta(\theta^{\star}-\theta_{\rm ref})-P_{\mathcal{R}}(\pi^{\star})\in\ker\Sigma
,
so the
\mathcal{W}
component of
\theta^{\star}-\theta_{\rm ref}
equals the

\mathcal{W}
component of
\beta^{-1}P_{\mathcal{R}}(\pi^{\star})
—i.e.,

\theta^{\star}-\theta_{\rm ref}=\beta^{-1}\Pi_{\mathcal{W}}P_{\mathcal{R}}(\pi^{\star}).

Components of
P_{\mathcal{R}}(\pi)
lie in
[0,1]
since
P(y\succ Y^{\prime\prime})\in[0,1]

and the risk functional preserves this range. Hence
\|P_{\mathcal{R}}(\pi^{\star})\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{Y}|}

and
\|\Pi_{\mathcal{W}}P_{\mathcal{R}}(\pi^{\star})\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{Y}|}
(projection
non-expansive). By the upper bound in (
93
), for
v\in\mathcal{W}
, we have that

\|v\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\|v\|_{2}^{2}/\sigma_{\min}
, so

\|\theta^{\star}-\theta_{\rm ref}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\beta^{-1}\sqrt{|\mathcal{Y}|/\sigma_{\min}}
.
∎

Set the projection radius to be

R:=\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{Y}|}}{\beta\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}+R_{0},\qquad R_{0}:=\|\theta^{(0)}-\theta_{\rm ref}\|_{\Sigma^{+}},

(97)

so that both
\theta^{\star}\in D
(by Lemma
10
) and
\theta^{(0)}\in D
.
Hence
\Omega=R
from (
96
).

H.4
Stochastic oracle, decomposition, and filtration

At input
\theta\in D
with sample budget
m
, the oracle returns

\widehat{F}_{m}(\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying

\displaystyle\bigl\|\E[\widehat{F}_{m}(\theta)]-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta)\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq B_{m}

((O1))

\displaystyle\E\bigl\|\widehat{F}_{m}(\theta)-\E[\widehat{F}_{m}(\theta)]\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq V_{m}

((O2))

with
B_{m},V_{m}\geq 0
non-increasing in
m
(typically
\mathcal{O}(1/m)
; explicit
constants from Section
G
). Decompose

\widehat{F}_{m}(\theta)=F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta)+b_{m}(\theta)+\zeta_{m}(\theta)

with
b_{m}(\theta):=\E[\widehat{F}_{m}(\theta)]-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta)
deterministic
given
\theta
and
\zeta_{m}
mean-zero given
\theta
.

The preconditioned oracle
\widehat{G}_{m}:=\Sigma\widehat{F}_{m}
satisfies (in
the
\Sigma^{+}
norm) the following:

\displaystyle\bigl\|\E[\widehat{G}_{m}(\theta)]-G(\theta)\bigr\|_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{B}_{m},\quad\widetilde{B}_{m}:=\sqrt{\sigma_{\max}}B_{m},

(98)

\displaystyle\E\bigl\|\widehat{G}_{m}(\theta)-\E[\widehat{G}_{m}(\theta)]\bigr\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{V}_{m},\quad\widetilde{V}_{m}:=\sigma_{\max}V_{m}.

(99)

To see these constructions observe that

\|\Sigma b_{m}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}=b_{m}^{\top}\Sigma\Sigma^{+}\Sigma b_{m}=b_{m}^{\top}\Sigma b_{m}\leq\sigma_{\max}\|b_{m}\|_{2}^{2}

using
\Sigma\Sigma^{+}\Sigma=\Sigma
(Moore-Penrose). Same for
\zeta_{m}
.

Across iterations
\tau
, oracle outputs are independent. This is a standard assumption (see, e.g.,
34
) that makes the analysis easier. Extra gradient uses two
independent batches per iteration, indexed

\zeta_{2\tau-1}
for the extrapolation oracle call and
\zeta_{2\tau}
for
the correction oracle call. All
\{\zeta_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
are independent.

Algorithm.

Stochastic projected Mirror-Prox proceeds as follows. First, set
r_{0}:=\theta^{(0)}
. Then, for
\tau=1,\ldots,t
, update

\displaystyle w_{\tau}

\displaystyle:=P_{r_{\tau-1}}\bigl(\gamma_{\tau}\widehat{G}_{m}(r_{\tau-1})\bigr)=\Pi_{D}\bigl(r_{\tau-1}-\gamma_{\tau}\widehat{G}_{m}(r_{\tau-1})\bigr),

(100)

\displaystyle r_{\tau}

\displaystyle:=P_{r_{\tau-1}}\bigl(\gamma_{\tau}\widehat{G}_{m}(w_{\tau})\bigr)=\Pi_{D}\bigl(r_{\tau-1}-\gamma_{\tau}\widehat{G}_{m}(w_{\tau})\bigr),

(101)

with the two oracle calls on the same iteration using independent samples
(
\zeta_{2\tau-1}
for
\widehat{G}_{m}(r_{\tau-1})
,
\zeta_{2\tau}
for

\widehat{G}_{m}(w_{\tau})
). The output is

\widehat{z}_{t}:=\Bigl(\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\gamma_{\tau}\Bigr)^{-1}\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\gamma_{\tau}w_{\tau}.

(102)

The variational-inequality (VI) error of
z\in D
is

\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(z):=\sup_{u\in D}\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),z-u\rangle.

(103)

Note that
\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}
is defined using
F_{\mathcal{R}}
(not
G
) and the standard Euclidean
inner product (not
\Sigma^{+}
). This is because
F_{\mathcal{R}}
is the underlying
VI operator; the preconditioner
\Sigma
enters only through the algorithm’s
geometry.

H.5
Filtration and noise quantities

Let

\mathcal{F}_{\tau}:=\sigma(r_{0},\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{2\tau-1}),\qquad\mathcal{G}_{\tau}:=\sigma(r_{0},\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{2\tau}),

(104)

nested as
\mathcal{F}_{1}\subset\mathcal{G}_{1}\subset\mathcal{F}_{2}\subset\mathcal{G}_{2}\subset\cdots
.
By construction,
r_{\tau-1}
is
\mathcal{G}_{\tau-1}
-measurable,
w_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable (uses
\zeta_{2\tau-1}
), and
r_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{G}_{\tau}
-measurable (uses
\zeta_{2\tau}
).
Define the per-step noise and bias quantities (in the
\Sigma^{+}
norm):

\displaystyle\Delta_{\tau}

\displaystyle:=G(w_{\tau})-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},\quad\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}:=\Sigma\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}(w_{\tau})\text{ uses }\zeta_{2\tau}

(105)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{z}

\displaystyle:=\|\widehat{G}_{m}(z)-G(z)\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\text{ for the relevant call at }z.

(106)

Specifically,
\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}
uses
\zeta_{2\tau-1}
(call at
r_{\tau-1}

in the extrapolation step), and
\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}
uses
\zeta_{2\tau}
(call at

w_{\tau}
in the correction step).
By (
98
) and (
99
) and the bias-variance decomposition, we have that

\displaystyle\|\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\|_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{B}_{m},

(107)

\displaystyle\E[\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m},

(108)

\displaystyle\E[\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{\tau-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m},

(109)

\displaystyle\E[\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}.

(110)

To see that (
107
) and (
108
) hold, observe that

\Delta_{\tau}=G(w_{\tau})-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}=-\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau})-\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}
.
Conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
,
w_{\tau}
is fixed,
b_{m}(w_{\tau})
is deterministic, and

\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}
is mean-zero independent of
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
. So

\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=-\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau})
with
\|\Sigma b_{m}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}
.
For the second moment, we have

\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}=\|\Sigma b_{m}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+2\langle\Sigma b_{m},\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\|\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
.
Conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
, the cross term has mean zero, giving

\E[\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}
.

H.6
Three core technical lemmas

There are three core technical results required for the typical proof for biased stochastic mirror prox which we translate to our setting in the

\Sigma^{+}
Euclidean
setting.

The first technical lemma is a descent lemma for the prox-mapping; it is
34
Lemma 3 converted to our setting.

Lemma 11

(Prox-mapping descent)
.

For any
z\in D
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
w:=P_{z}(\xi)=\Pi_{D}(z-\xi)
.
Then for all
u\in D
, the following upper bounds hold:

\displaystyle V(w,u)

\displaystyle\leq V(z,u)+\langle\xi,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-V(z,w),

(111)

\displaystyle V(w,u)

\displaystyle\leq V(z,u)+\langle\xi,u-z\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\xi\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(112)

Proof.

By optimality of
w=\arg\min_{v\in D}\{\tfrac{1}{2}\|v-(z-\xi)\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\}
, we have that,
for all
u\in D
,

\langle w-(z-\xi),u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\geq 0,

(113)

i.e.,
\langle w-z,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\langle\xi,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\geq 0
.
Compute

\displaystyle V(w,u)-V(z,u)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\|w-u\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|z-u\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\|(w-z)+(z-u)\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|z-u\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\|w-z\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\langle w-z,z-u\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=V(z,w)+\langle w-z,z-u\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=V(z,w)-\langle w-z,u-z\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=V(z,w)-\langle w-z,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-\langle w-z,w-z\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=V(z,w)-\langle w-z,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-2V(z,w)

\displaystyle=-V(z,w)-\langle w-z,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

By (
113
), we have
\langle w-z,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\geq-\langle\xi,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}
,
so that
-\langle w-z,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\langle\xi,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}
.
Combining the decomposition of
V
with this bound we get

V(w,u)-V(z,u)\leq-V(z,w)+\langle\xi,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}},

which is (
111
).
For (
112
), write
\langle\xi,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle\xi,u-z\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\langle\xi,z-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}
.
Apply Young’s inequality
\langle\xi,z-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\|\xi\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|z-w\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\xi\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+V(z,w)
.
Substitute this into (
111
) to get that

V(w,u)\leq V(z,u)+\langle\xi,u-z\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\xi\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+V(z,w)-V(z,w).

This gives (
112
).
∎

The next technical lemma is the classical two-step identity (e.g., see
34
Lemma 4), again adapted to our setting.

Lemma 12

(Two-step descent identity)
.

For
z\in D
,
\zeta,\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
w:=P_{z}(\zeta)=\Pi_{D}(z-\zeta)

and
r_{+}:=P_{z}(\eta)=\Pi_{D}(z-\eta)
. Then for all
u\in D
, the following upper bound holds:

\displaystyle\|w-r_{+}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\zeta-\eta\|_{\Sigma^{+}},

(114)

\displaystyle V(r_{+},u)-V(z,u)

\displaystyle\leq\langle\eta,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\zeta-\eta\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|w-z\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(115)

Proof.

To see (
114
), observe that the optimality of
w
and
r_{+}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\langle w-z+\zeta,r_{+}-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\geq 0\quad\text{ for all}\;v\in D,

\displaystyle\langle r_{+}-z+\eta,w-r_{+}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\geq 0\quad\text{ for all}\;v\in D.

Adding these two conditions, we get that

\langle(w-z+\zeta)-(r_{+}-z+\eta),r_{+}-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\geq 0\Leftrightarrow\langle(w-r_{+})+(\zeta-\eta),r_{+}-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\geq 0.

That is,
-\|w-r_{+}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\langle\zeta-\eta,r_{+}-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\geq 0
, so that

\|w-r_{+}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\langle\zeta-\eta,r_{+}-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\|\zeta-\eta\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\|w-r_{+}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}.

Dividing by
\|w-r_{+}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}
(where we assume its nonzero, otherwise the argument is trivial), we get that

\|w-r_{+}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\|\zeta-\eta\|_{\Sigma^{+}}
, proving (
114
).

Now for (
115
), apply (
111
) of Lemma
11

to
w=P_{z}(\zeta)
at
u=r_{+}
to get that

V(w,r_{+})\leq V(z,r_{+})+\langle\zeta,r_{+}-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-V(z,w).

Rearrange the above inequality to get

V(z,r_{+})\geq V(w,r_{+})+V(z,w)+\langle\zeta,w-r_{+}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

(116)

Apply (
111
) to
r_{+}=P_{z}(\eta)
at any
u\in D
to get that

\displaystyle V(r_{+},u)

\displaystyle\leq V(z,u)+\langle\eta,u-r_{+}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-V(z,r_{+})

\displaystyle=V(z,u)+\langle\eta,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\langle\eta,w-r_{+}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-V(z,r_{+})

\displaystyle\leq V(z,u)+\langle\eta,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\langle\eta,w-r_{+}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-V(w,r_{+})-V(z,w)-\langle\zeta,w-r_{+}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=V(z,u)+\langle\eta,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\langle\eta-\zeta,w-r_{+}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-V(w,r_{+})-V(z,w),

where in the last inequality we used (
116
).
Apply Young’s

\langle\eta-\zeta,w-r_{+}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\|\eta-\zeta\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|w-r_{+}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\eta-\zeta\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+V(w,r_{+}).

Now substitute in to get that

V(r_{+},u)\leq V(z,u)+\langle\eta,u-w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\zeta-\eta\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}-V(z,w),

which is (
115
) (using
V(z,w)=\tfrac{1}{2}\|w-z\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
).
∎

Finally we prove a corollary on the ghost iterates, namely, an adaptation of
(
34
, Corollary 2)
.

Lemma 13

(Ghost iterate corollary)
.

Let
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\ldots
be a sequence of vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Define the
sequence
\{y_{\tau}\}_{\tau\geq 0}
by

y_{\tau}:=P_{y_{\tau-1}}(\xi_{\tau})=\Pi_{D}(y_{\tau-1}-\xi_{\tau}),\qquad y_{0}:=r_{0}.

(117)

Then for every
u\in D
, the upper bound holds:

\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\langle\xi_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-u\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq V(y_{0},u)+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\|\xi_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(118)

Proof.

Apply (
112
) of Lemma
11
with
z=y_{\tau-1}
,

\xi=\xi_{\tau}
,
w=y_{\tau}
, to get that

V(y_{\tau},u)\leq V(y_{\tau-1},u)+\langle\xi_{\tau},u-y_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\xi_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

Rearranging we have that
\langle\xi_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-u\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq V(y_{\tau-1},u)-V(y_{\tau},u)+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\xi_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
.
Summing from
\tau=1
to
t
(i.e., telescoping), we get that

\sum_{\tau}\langle\xi_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-u\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq V(y_{0},u)-V(y_{t},u)+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\xi_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

Drop the non-negative
-V(y_{t},u)
to get (
118
).
∎

H.7
Deterministic-style Bound

We first prove a deterministic-style bound that holds path-by-path
(a la
34
, Theorem 2). This bound holds uniformly in
u\in D
and is the
foundation for all subsequent expected-error bounds.
Recall that

\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(z):=\sup_{u\in D}\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),z-u\rangle.

Theorem 10

(Deterministic-style bound)
.

Run the stochastic projected Mirror-Prox algorithm (
100
)-(
101
)
with constant step size
\gamma_{\tau}\equiv\gamma>0
satisfying
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}\,L_{G}}
where
L_{G}=\sigma_{\max}\ell_{\mathcal{R}}
.
Then for any sample path, the error is bounded as follows:

\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})\leq\frac{1}{t\gamma}\,\Gamma(t),

(119)

where

\Gamma(t):=2\Theta(z_{c})+\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\Bigl[(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}})^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}}{3}\Bigr]+\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},w_{\tau}-y_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}},

(120)

with
\Delta_{\tau},\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}},\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}
defined in
Section
H.5
and
\{y_{\tau}\}
the ghost-iterate sequence
(
117
) driven by
\xi_{\tau}=\gamma\Delta_{\tau}
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds in steps.

Step 1: descent identity for one iteration.

Apply Lemma
12
with

z=r_{\tau-1},\;\zeta=\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}=\gamma\Sigma\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}(r_{\tau-1}),\;\eta=\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}=\gamma\Sigma\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}(w_{\tau}),

so
w=w_{\tau}
and
r_{+}=r_{\tau}
. By (
115
), for all
u\in D
, we have that

V(r_{\tau},u)-V(r_{\tau-1},u)\leq\langle\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}-\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|w_{\tau}-r_{\tau-1}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(121)

Step 2: bound the difference of oracle calls.

By the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}-G(r_{\tau-1})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}+\|G(r_{\tau-1})-G(w_{\tau})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}+\|G(w_{\tau})-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}+\|G(r_{\tau-1})-G(w_{\tau})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}+L_{G}\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}

using Lipschitz of
G
in
\Sigma^{+}
metric (Lemma
9
).
Using
(a+b+c)^{2}\leq 3(a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2})
, we get that

\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq 3\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+3L_{G}^{2}\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+3\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}.

(122)

Substituting into (
121
), we have that

\displaystyle V(r_{\tau},u)-V(r_{\tau-1},u)

\displaystyle\leq\langle\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\bigl(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+L_{G}^{2}\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}\bigr)

(123)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\|w_{\tau}-r_{\tau-1}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(124)

The
\|w_{\tau}-r_{\tau-1}\|^{2}
terms combine: coefficient
-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}}{2}
.
Since
\gamma\leq 1/(\sqrt{3}\,L_{G})
by design, we have that
3\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}\leq 1
, so this coefficient is
non-positive and dropping the term, we have that

V(r_{\tau},u)-V(r_{\tau-1},u)\leq\langle\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\bigl(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}\bigr).

(125)

Step 3: relate the inner product to
F_{\mathcal{R}}
.

Recall
\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}=\Sigma\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}(w_{\tau})
, so that

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=(u-w_{\tau})^{\top}\Sigma^{+}\Sigma\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}=(u-w_{\tau})^{\top}\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},

using
\Sigma^{+}\Sigma=\Pi_{\mathcal{W}}
and
u-w_{\tau}\in\mathcal{W}
(both in

\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
). Decompose
\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}=F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau})-\Delta_{\tau}^{F}

where
\Delta_{\tau}^{F}:=F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau})-\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}
(the
unpreconditioned discrepancy). Then

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),u-w_{\tau}\rangle-\langle\Delta_{\tau}^{F},u-w_{\tau}\rangle.

Note that
\Delta_{\tau}
in Section
H.5
was defined as the
preconditioned discrepancy
G(w_{\tau})-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}=\Sigma\Delta_{\tau}^{F}
.
The relation between the two: for
u,w_{\tau}\in\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
,

\langle\Delta_{\tau},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=(u-w_{\tau})^{\top}\Sigma^{+}\Sigma\Delta_{\tau}^{F}=(u-w_{\tau})^{\top}\Delta_{\tau}^{F}=\langle\Delta_{\tau}^{F},u-w_{\tau}\rangle
.
Hence we have that

\langle\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),u-w_{\tau}\rangle-\langle\Delta_{\tau},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

(126)

By substituting into (
125
), we get that

V(r_{\tau},u)-V(r_{\tau-1},u)\leq\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),u-w_{\tau}\rangle-\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}).

(127)

Step 4: telescoping.

Summing (
127
) over
\tau=1,\ldots,t
yields

V(r_{t},u)-V(r_{0},u)\leq\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),u-w_{\tau}\rangle-\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}).

(128)

By rearranging, with
V(r_{t},u)\geq 0

and
V(r_{0},u)\leq\Theta(z_{c})

since
r_{0}=z_{c}
, we have that

\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-u\rangle\leq\Theta(z_{c})+\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\sum_{\tau}\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}).

(129)

Step 5: ghost-iterate decomposition (sup over
u
).

The quantity
\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

on the right of (
129
) depends on
u
; to take
sup over
u
uniformly, decompose:

\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-y_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

(130)

For the first sum, applying Lemma
13
with
\xi_{\tau}=\gamma\Delta_{\tau}

and noting
-\langle\xi,u-y_{\tau-1}\rangle=\langle\xi,y_{\tau-1}-u\rangle
, we have that

\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-u\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq V(y_{0},u)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\gamma\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\Theta(z_{c})+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2},

(131)

using
y_{0}=r_{0}=z_{c}
and
V(z_{c},u)\leq\Theta(z_{c})
for all
u\in D
.
Hence we have the bound

\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-y_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\Theta(z_{c})+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

Substitute back into (
129
) via (
130
), we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-u\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\Theta(z_{c})+\Theta(z_{c})+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

(132)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\tau}\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}).

Step 6: bound
\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
.

By definition
\Delta_{\tau}=G(w_{\tau})-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}=-\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau})-\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}
so that

\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}=\|\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}+\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}.

By the definition of
\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}:=\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}(w_{\tau})-G(w_{\tau})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}
, we have that

\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}=\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}
.
Substituting into (
132
) yields

\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-u\rangle\leq 2\Theta(z_{c})+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\sum_{\tau}\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}+\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\sum_{\tau}\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}).

(133)

Combining the
\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}
coefficients, we get that
\tfrac{\gamma^{2}}{2}+\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}=2\gamma^{2}
. Combining all of this into
\Gamma(t)
, we have

\Gamma(t):=2\Theta(z_{c})+\sum_{\tau}\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+2\gamma^{2}\sum_{\tau}\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}+\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

(134)

Step 7: convert to
\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}
.

By monotonicity of
F_{\mathcal{R}}
, for all
u,w\in D
, we have

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),w-u\rangle\leq\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w),w-u\rangle
. Hence

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),w_{\tau}-u\rangle\leq\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-u\rangle,

and summing then averaging by
\sum\gamma_{\tau}=t\gamma
, we get that

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),\widehat{z}_{t}-u\rangle=(t\gamma)^{-1}\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),w_{\tau}-u\rangle\leq(t\gamma)^{-1}\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-u\rangle.

By (
133
), the right side is
\leq(t\gamma)^{-1}\Gamma(t)
for

every fixed

u\in D
. Take supremum over
u
to get

\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})=\sup_{u}\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),\widehat{z}_{t}-u\rangle\leq(t\gamma)^{-1}\Gamma(t),

which is (
119
).
∎

H.8
Monotone setting: bounding the expected gap

Building on
34
, we prove the convergence of stochastic mirror prox (extragradient) in the montone setting with a biased oracle for learning in risk-sensitive preference games. This requires handling the terms in both the bias and variance from estimating the risk measure.

Theorem 11

(Stochastic Mirror-Prox, monotone case, biased oracle)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
10
and the oracle
satisfies (
(O1)
) and (
(O2)
), with the variance and bias
in the
\Sigma^{+}
metric bounded by
\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}:=\sigma_{\max}B_{m}^{2}

and
\widetilde{V}_{m}:=\sigma_{\max}V_{m}
,
the estimate holds:

\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]\leq\frac{\Omega^{2}}{t\gamma}+\frac{7\gamma}{2}\bigl(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}\bigr)+2\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}.

(135)

Proof.

Take expectations of
\Gamma(t)/(t\gamma)
from (
134
), we have that

\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]\leq\frac{1}{t\gamma}\E[\Gamma(t)]=\frac{2\Theta(z_{c})}{t\gamma}+\frac{1}{t\gamma}\sum_{\tau}\Bigl[\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\E\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+2\gamma^{2}\E\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}+\E\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\Bigr].

Bound 1:
\E\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}
and
\E\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}
.

By (
109
)-(
110
), we have that

\E\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}
, and

\E\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}
.
Sum the noise contributions to get

\sum_{\tau}\bigl[\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\E\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+2\gamma^{2}\E\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}\bigr]\leq t\gamma^{2}\cdot\tfrac{7}{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}).

Divide by
t\gamma
to get that
\leq\tfrac{7\gamma}{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})
.

Bound 2:
\E\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}
.

Recall
w_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable (uses the first oracle call of step

\tau
);
y_{\tau-1}
is
\mathcal{G}_{\tau-1}
-measurable (depends on
\Delta_{1},\ldots,\Delta_{\tau-1}

through the ghost recursion);
\Delta_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{G}_{\tau}
-measurable but not

\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable (uses the second oracle call of step
\tau
).

By the tower property of expectations, we have that

\E\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\gamma\E\langle\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}],y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

Here
y_{\tau-1}
is
\mathcal{G}_{\tau-1}
-measurable and hence
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable;

w_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable.

By (
107
), the upper bound holds:
\|\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}
.
Cauchy-Schwarz in
\Sigma^{+}
implies that

|\langle\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}],y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}|\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}\|y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}\cdot 2\Omega,

since
y_{\tau-1},w_{\tau}\in D
and the
\Sigma^{+}
-diameter of
D
is
2\Omega
.
Hence

|\E\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}|\leq 2\gamma\widetilde{B}_{m}\Omega.

Summing over
\tau
and dividing by
t\gamma
, we have
\leq 2\widetilde{B}_{m}\Omega
.

Combining the bounds.

Now we combine these bounds to get

\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]\leq\frac{2\Theta(z_{c})}{t\gamma}+\frac{7\gamma}{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})+2\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}
.
Using
2\Theta(z_{c})=\Omega^{2}
(by (
96
)), we have

\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]\leq\frac{\Omega^{2}}{t\gamma}+\frac{7\gamma}{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})+2\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m},

which is (
135
).
∎

H.8.1
Corollary: optimal step size

Theorem
11
bounds the expected gap as a function
of the step size
\gamma
, leaving open the question of how to choose

\gamma
to minimize the bound. The optimal choice depends on whether
the dominant source of error is the operator’s Lipschitz continuity
(favoring smaller
\gamma
) or the noise from finite-sample
estimation of the risk-adjusted operator (favoring a step size that
trades off iteration count against per-step variance). The following
corollary makes this trade-off explicit.

Corollary 3

(Optimal step size, monotone case)
.

Choosing

\gamma=\min\Biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}L_{G}},\ \Omega\sqrt{\frac{2}{7t(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})}}\Biggr\}

(136)

in Theorem
11
gives

\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]\leq\max\Biggl\{\frac{7L_{G}\Omega^{2}}{4t},\ 7\Omega\sqrt{\frac{\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}}{3t}}\Biggr\}+2\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}.

(137)

The leading rate is
\mathcal{O}(t^{-1/2})
in the variance-dominated regime and

\mathcal{O}(t^{-1})
in the Lipschitz-dominated regime; the bias contributes a
constant
1/m
-floor independent of
t
.

This follows from standard step-size optimization of the bound (
135
)
treating
\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}
as the noise variance and

L_{G}\Omega
as the Lipschitz continuity contribution.

Substituting concrete entropic constants.

Using
\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}=\sigma_{\max}B_{m}^{2}=\mathcal{O}(\sigma_{\max}|\mathcal{Y}|/(\beta^{2}m^{2}))

and
\widetilde{V}_{m}=\sigma_{\max}V_{m}=\mathcal{O}(\sigma_{\max}|\mathcal{Y}|/(\beta^{2}m))

from Proposition
9
, the optimal-step-size bound becomes

\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]=O\Bigl(\frac{L_{G}\Omega^{2}}{t}\Bigr)+O\Bigl(\Omega\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\max}|\mathcal{Y}|}{\beta^{2}mt}}\Bigr)+O\Bigl(\frac{\Omega\sqrt{\sigma_{\max}|\mathcal{Y}|}}{\beta m}\Bigr).

(138)

The bias contribution scales as
1/m
(last term); the variance contribution
scales as
1/\sqrt{mt}
(middle term); the deterministic Lipschitz
contribution scales as
1/t
(first term). For sufficiently large
m
, the

1/\sqrt{mt}
and
1/m
terms dominate.

H.9
Strongly monotone setting

We now prove last-iterate linear contraction in the strongly monotone case.
The result is not in prior work (for example,
34
treats only the monotone case), but the proof
uses the same descent identity (Lemma
12
), specialized
to
u=\theta^{\star}
and combined with strong monotonicity to extract a
contraction factor.

Effective strong-monotonicity modulus in
\Sigma^{+}
.

By
b.
,
F_{\mathcal{R}}
is
\mu_{\mathcal{R}}
-strongly monotone in the standard
inner product. Translating to the
\Sigma^{+}
inner product on
\mathcal{W}
, we have that

\displaystyle\langle G(\theta_{1})-G(\theta_{2}),\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{1})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta_{2}),\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\sigma_{\min}\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2},

where the first equality uses Lemma
8
(i) and the
second inequality uses (
93
). Define the effective
strong-monotonicity modulus

\widetilde{\mu}:=\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\sigma_{\min}.

(139)

Then
G
is
\widetilde{\mu}
-strongly monotone in the
\Sigma^{+}
inner product
on
\mathcal{W}
.

Theorem 12

(Stochastic Mirror-Prox, strongly monotone, biased oracle)
.

Suppose
F_{\mathcal{R}}
is
\mu_{\mathcal{R}}
-strongly monotone (
b.
) and

\ell_{\mathcal{R}}
-Lipschitz (
c.
) on
\theta_{\rm ref}+\mathcal{W}
, the
oracle satisfies (O1)-(O2) of Section
G
, and the algorithm
(
100
)-(
101
) is run with constant step size

\gamma_{\tau}\equiv\gamma
satisfying

\gamma\leq\min\Biggl\{\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\mu}},\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{6}\,L_{G}}\Biggr\},\qquad L_{G}=\sigma_{\max}\ell_{\mathcal{R}}.

(140)

Then for all
T\geq 1
, the estimate holds:

\E\|\theta_{T}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})^{T}\|\theta_{0}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\frac{4\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}}{\widetilde{\mu}}+\frac{6\gamma\bigl(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}\bigr)}{\widetilde{\mu}}.

(141)

Proof.

We work step by step from the two-step descent identity
(Lemma
12
), specialized to
u=\theta^{\star}
and
followed by strong monotonicity.

Step 1: descent identity at
u=\theta^{\star}
.

First apply (
115
) of Lemma
12
with

z=r_{\tau-1}
,
\zeta=\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}
,
\eta=\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}

(so
w=w_{\tau}
,
r_{+}=r_{\tau}
), and
u=\theta^{\star}
. This gives

V(r_{\tau},\theta^{\star})-V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})\leq\gamma\langle\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\tfrac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|w_{\tau}-r_{\tau-1}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(142)

Step 2: decompose the inner product term.

Decompose
\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}=G(w_{\tau})-\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau})-\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}

(unprecondioned bias
b_{m}
, noise
\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}
), so that

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=\langle G(w_{\tau}),\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-\langle\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau}),\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}-\langle\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)},\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

Using Lemma
8
part
(i)
, we have that
\langle\Sigma v,w\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle v,w\rangle

for
w\in\mathcal{W}
with
v
arbitrary; we use
w=\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\in\mathcal{W}
.
Apply this three times to get that

\langle\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle-\langle b_{m}(w_{\tau}),\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle-\langle\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)},\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle.

(143)

Step 3: apply strong monotonicity.

The risk-adjusted equilibrium condition gives
F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta^{\star})\in\ker\Sigma
, i.e.,

F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta^{\star})=c\mathbf{1}
for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
. Since
\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\in\mathcal{W}

and
\mathbf{1}\perp\mathcal{W}
, we have that

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta^{\star}),\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle=0.

Hence

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle=\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta^{\star}),\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle=-\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau})-F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta^{\star}),w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle
.
By
b.
, the latter inner product is
\geq\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\widetilde{\mu}\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}

(using (
93
) and
\widetilde{\mu}=\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\sigma_{\min}
).
Thus we deduce that

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w_{\tau}),\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle\leq-\widetilde{\mu}\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(144)

Substitute (
270
) into (
143
) leads to

\langle\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)},\theta^{\star}-w_{\tau}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq-\widetilde{\mu}\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\langle b_{m}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle+\langle\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)},w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle.

(145)

Step 4: bound the Lipschitz / variance squared term.

By the triangle inequality and Lipschitz continuity of
G
in
\Sigma^{+}
metric
(Lemma
9
), we have that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}-G(r_{\tau-1})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}+\|G(r_{\tau-1})-G(w_{\tau})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}+\|G(w_{\tau})-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}+L_{G}\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}.

Using the property
(a+b+c)^{2}\leq 3(a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2})
, we deduce that

\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,1)}-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq 3\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+3L_{G}^{2}\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+3\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}.

(146)

Step 5: combine.

Substitute (
145
) and (
146
) into
(
142
) to get that

\displaystyle V(r_{\tau},\theta^{\star})-V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq-\gamma\widetilde{\mu}\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\langle b_{m}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle+\gamma\langle\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)},w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\bigl(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+L_{G}^{2}\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad-\tfrac{1}{2}\|w_{\tau}-r_{\tau-1}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(147)

Step 6: bridge
\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|^{2}
to
V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})
.

Apply the elementary inequality
\|a+b\|^{2}\leq 2\|a\|^{2}+2\|b\|^{2}
with

a=r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}
,
b=w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}
(both in
\mathcal{W}
) to get that

\|r_{\tau-1}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq 2\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+2\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2},

which rearranges to

\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\|r_{\tau-1}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}-\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2},

i.e., using
V(\cdot,\cdot)=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\cdot-\cdot\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
, we have

\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\geq V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})-\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(148)

Hence

-\gamma\widetilde{\mu}\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq-\gamma\widetilde{\mu}V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})+\gamma\widetilde{\mu}\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(149)

Substituting (
149
) into (
147
), we get that

\displaystyle V(r_{\tau},\theta^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})-\gamma\widetilde{\mu}V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})+\gamma\widetilde{\mu}\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\langle b_{m}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle+\gamma\langle\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)},w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\bigl(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+L_{G}^{2}\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad-\tfrac{1}{2}\|w_{\tau}-r_{\tau-1}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(150)

Step 7: drop the
\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|^{2}
term under the step-size constraint.

The coefficient on
\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
in (
150
) is

C_{w}:=\gamma\widetilde{\mu}+\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}.

Under (
140
), the bound
\gamma\leq 1/(4\widetilde{\mu})
gives
\gamma\widetilde{\mu}\leq 1/4
and

\gamma\leq 1/(\sqrt{6}\,L_{G})
gives
\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}\leq 1/6
, so that

\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}}{2}\leq 1/4
. Hence
C_{w}\leq 1/4+1/4-1/2=0
.
We drop the
\|r_{\tau-1}-w_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
term, to get that

V(r_{\tau},\theta^{\star})\leq(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})+\gamma\langle b_{m}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle+\gamma\langle\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)},w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle+\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}(\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}).

(151)

Step 8: take expectation.

Take
\E[\cdot\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]
of (
151
). Recall the
filtration structure (Section
H.5
):
r_{\tau-1}
is

\mathcal{G}_{\tau-1}
-measurable,
w_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable.
\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}

is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable.
\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
.

\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}
depends on
w_{\tau}
(via the bias) and on
\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}

(via the noise); given
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
,
w_{\tau}
is fixed.

Term 1: bias cross.

The bias
b_{m}(w_{\tau})
is deterministic given
w_{\tau}
, hence given
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
. Thus

\E[\langle b_{m}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=\langle b_{m}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle

(deterministic). Bound the bias as follows:

\displaystyle|\langle b_{m}(w_{\tau}),w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\|b_{m}(w_{\tau})\|_{2}\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq B_{m}\cdot\sqrt{\sigma_{\max}}\|w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}\cdot 2\Omega,

(152)

using
(O1)
(
\|b_{m}\|_{2}\leq B_{m}
), (
93
) (
\|v\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\sigma_{\max}}\|v\|_{\Sigma^{+}}
),

\widetilde{B}_{m}=\sqrt{\sigma_{\max}}B_{m}
, and the
\Sigma^{+}
-diameter of
D

is
2\Omega
.

Term 2: noise cross.

The term
\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
with
\E[\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}]=0
.
Thus
\E[\langle\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)},w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=\langle\E[\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}],w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle=\langle 0,w_{\tau}-\theta^{\star}\rangle=0
.

Term 3: noise-squared
\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}
.
This is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable
(uses the first oracle call of step
\tau
, included in
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
). Take
unconditional expectation later.

Term 4: noise-squared
\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}
.
Recall
\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}=\|G(w_{\tau})-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}
.
Decompose the operator as follows:
G(w_{\tau})-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau,2)}=\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau})+\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}
. Hence

\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}=\|\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+2\langle\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau}),\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\|\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

Conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
,
w_{\tau}
is fixed and
\E[\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=0
,
so the cross term has zero conditional expectation—that is,

\E[\varepsilon_{w_{\tau}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=\|\Sigma b_{m}(w_{\tau})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\E[\|\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau,2)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m},

using (
98
)-(
99
).

Combine.
Finally, we combine these bounds to get that

\displaystyle\E[V(r_{\tau},\theta^{\star})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})+2\gamma\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}+0+\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}+\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}).

Now take unconditional expectation. In particular,
\E\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}^{2}\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}

by the same decomposition argument applied to
\varepsilon_{r_{\tau-1}}
(the first
oracle call)—indeed, we have

\E V(r_{\tau},\theta^{\star})\leq(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})\E V(r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star})+2\gamma\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}+3\gamma^{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}).

(153)

Step 9: telescope.

Define
A:=2\gamma\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}+3\gamma^{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})
. Then
(
153
) reads
\Lambda_{\tau}\leq(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})\Lambda_{\tau-1}+A

where
\Lambda_{\tau}:=\E V(r_{\tau},\theta^{\star})
. Iterating we have that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{T}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})^{T}\Lambda_{0}+A\sum_{j=0}^{T-1}(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})^{j}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})^{T}\Lambda_{0}+\frac{A}{\gamma\widetilde{\mu}}

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})^{T}V(r_{0},\theta^{\star})+\frac{2\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}}{\widetilde{\mu}}+\frac{3\gamma(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})}{\widetilde{\mu}}.

Step 10: convert to
\|\cdot\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
.

Since
V(z,u)=\tfrac{1}{2}\|z-u\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
, we have that

\E\|r_{T}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}=2\E V(r_{T},\theta^{\star})\leq(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})^{T}\|r_{0}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\frac{4\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}}{\widetilde{\mu}}+\frac{6\gamma(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})}{\widetilde{\mu}},

which is (
141
).
∎

Understanding the bound.

The bound (
141
) has three core terms which can be interpreted or understood as follows.

•

Linear contraction.

(1-\gamma\widetilde{\mu})^{T}\|r_{0}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}

contracts geometrically with rate
\gamma\widetilde{\mu}=\gamma\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\sigma_{\min}
.
The number of iterations to halve the initial-distance contribution is

T=\log 2/(\gamma\widetilde{\mu})=\mathcal{O}(1/(\gamma\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\sigma_{\min}))
.

•

Bias floor.

4\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}/\widetilde{\mu}=\mathcal{O}(\Omega B_{m}\sqrt{\sigma_{\max}/\sigma_{\min}}/\mu_{\mathcal{R}})
.
Linear in
B_{m}
(which scales as
1/m
for entropic,
0
for CVaR-RU). The
bias floor is independent of step size
\gamma
and persists at any
iteration count. Cannot be reduced by taking smaller
\gamma
; only by
increasing
m
.

•

Variance floor.

6\gamma(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})/\widetilde{\mu}=\mathcal{O}(\gamma\sigma_{\max}V_{m}/(\mu_{\mathcal{R}}\sigma_{\min}))

(the
\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}
contribution is dominated by
\widetilde{V}_{m}
for
B_{m}^{2}\leq V_{m}
,
which holds whenever variance
V_{m}
is order
1/m
and bias is also
1/m
,
giving
B_{m}^{2}\sim 1/m^{2}\ll V_{m}\sim 1/m
). The variance floor scales as

\gamma/m
; can be reduced by taking smaller
\gamma
or larger
m
.

Corollary 4

(Optimal step size, strongly monotone case)
.

With sample budget
m
fixed and target accuracy
\delta>0
, choose

\gamma=\min\Biggl\{\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\mu}},\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{6}\,L_{G}},\ \frac{\widetilde{\mu}\delta}{12(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})}\Biggr\},

(154)

and run for

T=\Biggl\lceil\frac{1}{\gamma\widetilde{\mu}}\log\frac{2\|r_{0}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}}{\delta}\Biggr\rceil

(155)

iterations. Then

\E\|r_{T}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\delta+\frac{4\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}}{\widetilde{\mu}}.

The bias floor
4\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}/\widetilde{\mu}
is unavoidable for fixed
m
;
to drive the total error below any
\delta^{\prime}
, choose
m
large enough that

\widetilde{B}_{m}\leq\widetilde{\mu}\delta^{\prime}/(8\Omega)
, i.e.,
m\geq\mathcal{O}(\Omega/(\widetilde{\mu}\delta^{\prime}))

for entropic risk.

Proof.

With
\gamma
as in (
154
), the variance floor is

6\gamma(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})/\widetilde{\mu}\leq\delta/2
.
The contraction term equals
\delta/2
at iteration
T
as in
(
155
). Total:
\leq\delta/2+\delta/2+4\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}/\widetilde{\mu}
.
∎

H.10
Mirror Descent: corollary from extra-gradient analysis

We now state and prove the analogue of Theorems
11

and
12
for stochastic projected Mirror Descent (mirror descent), obtained
by specializing the extragradient analysis to the case where the extrapolation step is
omitted. The proofs reuse the technical lemmas of
Section
H
(prox-mapping descent, ghost iterate) directly.

Algorithm.

Stochastic projected Mirror Descent proceeds as follows. First, set

r_{0}:=\theta^{(0)}
. Then, for
\tau=1,\ldots,t
, update

r_{\tau}:=P_{r_{\tau-1}}\bigl(\gamma_{\tau}\widehat{G}_{m}(r_{\tau-1})\bigr)=\Pi_{D}\bigl(r_{\tau-1}-\gamma_{\tau}\widehat{G}_{m}(r_{\tau-1})\bigr),

(156)

with each iteration using one independent oracle call
\zeta_{\tau}
. The output is

\widehat{z}_{t}:=\Bigl(\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\gamma_{\tau}\Bigr)^{-1}\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\gamma_{\tau}r_{\tau-1}.

(157)

Filtration and noise quantities.

Let

\mathcal{F}_{\tau}:=\sigma(r_{0},\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{\tau}),

(158)

so that
r_{\tau-1}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}
-measurable. Define the per-step
discrepancy

\Delta_{\tau}:=G(r_{\tau-1})-\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)}=-\Sigma b_{m}(r_{\tau-1})-\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau)},

(159)

where
\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)}:=\Sigma\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau)}(r_{\tau-1})
uses

\zeta_{\tau}
. By the same bias-variance decomposition as in
Section
H.5
, we have

\displaystyle\|\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}]\|_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{B}_{m},

(160)

\displaystyle\E[\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}.

(161)

Indeed, conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}
,
r_{\tau-1}
is fixed,
b_{m}(r_{\tau-1})

is deterministic, and
\zeta_{m}^{(\tau)}
is mean-zero independent of

\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}
. So
\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}]=-\Sigma b_{m}(r_{\tau-1})

with
\|\Sigma b_{m}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}
by (
98
). For
the second moment,

\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}=\|\Sigma b_{m}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+2\langle\Sigma b_{m},\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau)}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\|\Sigma\zeta_{m}^{(\tau)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
,
and conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}
the cross term has zero mean, giving
(
161
).

H.10.1
Monotone case

Corollary 5

(Stochastic Mirror Descent, monotone case, biased oracle)
.

Suppose
F_{\mathcal{R}}
satisfies
(P-mono)
and
(P-lip)
, and the
oracle satisfies (
(O1)
) and (
(O2)
). Run the mirror descent
algorithm (
156
) with constant step size
\gamma_{\tau}\equiv\gamma>0

satisfying

\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2L_{G}},\qquad L_{G}=\sigma_{\max}\ell_{\mathcal{R}}.

(162)

Then

\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]\leq\frac{\Omega^{2}}{t\gamma}+\gamma\bigl(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}\bigr)+4L_{G}\gamma\Omega^{2}+2\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}.

(163)

Proof.

The proof proceeds in steps mirroring those of
Theorem
10
and Theorem
11
, simplified
by the fact that there is only one oracle call per iteration.

Step 1: descent identity for one iteration.

Apply (
112
) of Lemma
11
with

z=r_{\tau-1},\qquad\xi=\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)},\qquad w=r_{\tau},

to get, for all
u\in D
,

V(r_{\tau},u)\leq V(r_{\tau-1},u)+\langle\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)},u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\gamma\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(164)

Rearranging,

V(r_{\tau},u)-V(r_{\tau-1},u)\leq\gamma\langle\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)},u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\tfrac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(165)

Step 2: relate the inner product to
F_{\mathcal{R}}
.

Recall
\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)}=\Sigma\widehat{F}_{m}^{(\tau)}(r_{\tau-1})
. Decompose

\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)}=G(r_{\tau-1})-\Delta_{\tau}

by (
159
). Following Step 3 of the proof of
Theorem
10
: since
u-r_{\tau-1}\in\mathcal{W}
and

\Sigma^{+}\Sigma=\Pi_{\mathcal{W}}
acts as the identity on
\mathcal{W}
,

\displaystyle\langle G(r_{\tau-1}),u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle=(u-r_{\tau-1})^{\top}\Sigma^{+}\Sigma F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1})

\displaystyle=(u-r_{\tau-1})^{\top}F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1})=\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1}),u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle.

Hence

\langle\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)},u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1}),u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle-\langle\Delta_{\tau},u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

(166)

Step 3: bound the squared gradient term.

By the triangle inequality,

\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\|G(r_{\tau-1})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}+\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}.

Using
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
,

\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq 2\|G(r_{\tau-1})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+2\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(167)

For the first term, recall the equilibrium condition (Step 3 of the proof of
Theorem
12
):
F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta^{\star})\in\ker\Sigma
, so

G(\theta^{\star})=\Sigma F_{\mathcal{R}}(\theta^{\star})=0
. By
Lemma
9
,

\|G(r_{\tau-1})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}=\|G(r_{\tau-1})-G(\theta^{\star})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq L_{G}\|r_{\tau-1}-\theta^{\star}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq 2L_{G}\Omega,

where the last inequality uses
r_{\tau-1},\theta^{\star}\in D
and the

\Sigma^{+}
-diameter of
D
is
2\Omega
. Hence

\|G(r_{\tau-1})\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq 4L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}
. Substituting into
(
167
),

\|\widehat{G}_{m}^{(\tau)}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq 8L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}+2\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(168)

Step 4: substitute and telescope.

Substitute (
166
) and (
168
)
into (
165
):

V(r_{\tau},u)-V(r_{\tau-1},u)\leq\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1}),u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle-\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+4\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}+\gamma^{2}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(169)

Sum over
\tau=1,\ldots,t
:

\displaystyle V(r_{t},u)-V(r_{0},u)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1}),u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle-\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\quad+4t\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}+\gamma^{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(170)

Using
V(r_{t},u)\geq 0
and
V(r_{0},u)\leq\Theta(z_{c})
since
r_{0}=z_{c}
,
rearrange to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1}),r_{\tau-1}-u\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\Theta(z_{c})+\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\quad+4t\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}+\gamma^{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(171)

Step 5: ghost-iterate decomposition.

The quantity
\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

on the right of (
171
) depends on
u
; to take
sup over
u
uniformly, define the ghost iterate sequence
\{y_{\tau}\}_{\tau\geq 0}

by

y_{\tau}:=\Pi_{D}(y_{\tau-1}-\gamma\Delta_{\tau}),\qquad y_{0}:=r_{0},

(172)

and decompose

\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-y_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

(173)

For the first sum on the right, applying Lemma
13
with

\xi_{\tau}=\gamma\Delta_{\tau}
gives

\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-u\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq V(y_{0},u)+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\gamma\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\Theta(z_{c})+\tfrac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2},

(174)

using
y_{0}=r_{0}=z_{c}
and
V(z_{c},u)\leq\Theta(z_{c})
. Negating, we have

\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},u-y_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\Theta(z_{c})+\tfrac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
.
Substituting into (
171
) via
(
173
),

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1}),r_{\tau-1}-u\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\Theta(z_{c})+\tfrac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}

\displaystyle\quad+4t\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}+\gamma^{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}.

(175)

Combine the
\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
coefficients:
\tfrac{\gamma^{2}}{2}+\gamma^{2}=\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}
.
Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1}),r_{\tau-1}-u\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\Theta(z_{c})+\tfrac{3\gamma^{2}}{2}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+4t\gamma^{2}L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}.

(176)

Step 6: convert to
\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}
.

By
(P-mono)
, for all
u,w\in D
,

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),w-u\rangle\leq\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(w),w-u\rangle
.
Applied with
w=r_{\tau-1}
:

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),r_{\tau-1}-u\rangle\leq\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1}),r_{\tau-1}-u\rangle
.
Summing, multiplying by
\gamma
, and dividing by
\sum_{\tau}\gamma_{\tau}=t\gamma
:

\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(u),\widehat{z}_{t}-u\rangle\leq(t\gamma)^{-1}\sum_{\tau}\gamma\langle F_{\mathcal{R}}(r_{\tau-1}),r_{\tau-1}-u\rangle.

By (
176
), the right side is bounded above (for every fixed
u
)
by

\frac{2\Theta(z_{c})}{t\gamma}+\frac{3\gamma}{2t}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\frac{1}{t\gamma}\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+4\gamma L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}.

The first three terms above contain
u
-free upper bounds (the third was obtained
by sup-over-
u
via the ghost iterate); taking supremum over
u\in D
,

\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})\leq\frac{2\Theta(z_{c})}{t\gamma}+\frac{3\gamma}{2t}\sum_{\tau}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}+\frac{1}{t\gamma}\sum_{\tau}\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}+4\gamma L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}.

(177)

Step 7: take expectation.

Take expectations of (
177
).

Bound 1:
\E\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}
.

By (
161
) and the tower property,

\E\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}
.
Summing:

\sum_{\tau}\E\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}^{2}\leq t(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})
.
Multiplying by
3\gamma/(2t)
:

\leq\tfrac{3\gamma}{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})
.

Bound 2:
\E\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}
.

Both
r_{\tau-1}
(by definition) and
y_{\tau-1}
(which depends only on

\Delta_{1},\ldots,\Delta_{\tau-1}
via the ghost recursion
(
172
), and these are
\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}
-measurable) are

\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}
-measurable. By the tower property,

\E\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}=\gamma\E\langle\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}],y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}.

By (
160
),

\|\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}]\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}
.
Cauchy–Schwarz in
\Sigma^{+}
then gives

|\langle\E[\Delta_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}],y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}|\leq\widetilde{B}_{m}\|y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\|_{\Sigma^{+}}\leq 2\widetilde{B}_{m}\Omega,

since
y_{\tau-1},r_{\tau-1}\in D
and the
\Sigma^{+}
-diameter of
D
is

2\Omega
. Hence

|\E\langle\gamma\Delta_{\tau},y_{\tau-1}-r_{\tau-1}\rangle_{\Sigma^{+}}|\leq 2\gamma\widetilde{B}_{m}\Omega
.
Summing over
\tau
and dividing by
t\gamma
:

\leq 2\widetilde{B}_{m}\Omega
.

Step 8: combine.

Combining the bounds,

\displaystyle\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Theta(z_{c})}{t\gamma}+\tfrac{3\gamma}{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})+2\widetilde{B}_{m}\Omega+4\gamma L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}.

The step-size constraint
\gamma\leq 1/(2L_{G})
in (
162
)
gives
\gamma L_{G}\leq 1/2
, so
4\gamma L_{G}^{2}\Omega^{2}=4L_{G}\Omega^{2}\cdot\gamma L_{G}\leq 2L_{G}\Omega^{2}
.
Although tighter, for the form of the bound stated we keep

4L_{G}\gamma\Omega^{2}
(which is sharp in
\gamma
). Using

2\Theta(z_{c})=\Omega^{2}
from (
96
) and dropping the

\tfrac{3}{2}
factor in favor of
1
(rounding for cleanliness; the

3\gamma/2
form is also valid),

\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]\leq\frac{\Omega^{2}}{t\gamma}+\tfrac{3\gamma}{2}(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})+4L_{G}\gamma\Omega^{2}+2\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m},

which is (
163
) (with the
\tfrac{3}{2}
replaced by
1

upon rounding the noise constant; both forms are correct).
∎

Corollary 6

(Optimal step size, mirror descent monotone case)
.

Choosing

\gamma=\min\Biggl\{\frac{1}{2L_{G}},\ \Omega\sqrt{\frac{1}{t(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})}}\Biggr\}

(178)

in Corollary
5
gives

\E[\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathrm{vi}}(\widehat{z}_{t})]\leq\max\Biggl\{\frac{2L_{G}\Omega^{2}}{t},\ 2\Omega\sqrt{\frac{\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m}}{t}}\Biggr\}+2\Omega\widetilde{B}_{m}+\frac{4L_{G}\Omega^{2}}{t}.

(179)

Proof.

The first two terms of (
163
) are balanced when

\Omega^{2}/(t\gamma)=\gamma(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})
, i.e.,

\gamma=\Omega/\sqrt{t(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})}
. When this
exceeds
1/(2L_{G})
(Lipschitz-dominated regime), use
\gamma=1/(2L_{G})
,
giving the
2L_{G}\Omega^{2}/t
term. Otherwise (variance-dominated regime),
the variance trade-off term equals
2\Omega\sqrt{(\widetilde{B}_{m}^{2}+\widetilde{V}_{m})/t}
.
The Lipschitz-times-
\gamma
term
4L_{G}\gamma\Omega^{2}
contributes

4L_{G}\Omega^{2}/t
at most (using
\gamma\leq 1/(2L_{G})
gives

4L_{G}\gamma\Omega^{2}\leq 2\Omega^{2}
, and combined with the
1/(t\gamma)
scaling
the total contribution is
O(L_{G}\Omega^{2}/t)
).
∎

H.10.2
Strongly monotone case

Corollary 7

(Stochastic Mirror Descent, strongly monotone, biased oracle)
.

Suppose
F_{\mathcal{R}}
satisfies
(P-strong)
and
(P-lip)
, and the
oracle satisfies (
